Receta para hacer Penetes

Tomar palos de salçe limpios y quimallos en una
chimenea limpia, y como se hiçiere brassa, matalla,
en vna vaveña bedriada que tenga aqua Rosada, y de
flor de naranja, y de murta, y poner lo an asecar,
donde no le de el sol; y despues de seco, molerlo an y
paseallo por un cedazo sutil, y para haçerlos penetes,
tomar an dos honças de alquitira muy blanca, y puesta
en vna olla nueba, con mucha aqua Rosada; tomaran
veinte onças deste carbon molido, y una libra de menguy,
y media de estoraque en poluo, y una conca de Ambar
con otra de Almisque, todo hecho en poluo, y amasarlo
an todo con alquitira, muy bien amaseado, y haran
los Penetes muy bien amassados del tamano que 6
quisieren, y puestos en una tabla limpia cubierta con
vna toalla dexarlo an secar en casea, y despues de muy
gvardarlos an, y quanto mais aneios, son menores, y
si quisieren que no Ardan tanto echaran no mas de
dies y seys honças de carbon, y vna de Algabia

Otra manera de haçer
Peuetes

Tomar dos libros de menjuy, y vna de estoraque
muy bien molido, y paseado por vn çedazo sutil,
y una onça de Ambar, y otra de algabia, y media
de Almiscle, y libra y media de carbon de salçe quimado
y molido de la manera que en la otra receta diçe, y
passado por el çedazo un chiran esta carbon y
meniuy, y estoraque, en vna Varena Vidriada, y 2
echaran alquitira asta quatro onças, loquat estara
en vemoio en agua rosada, y de murta, en vna
olla vidriada grande y al cauo di cineo o seys dias
que estubiere en remoxo cota lo an por vn panno o
çedaço algo ralo iuntar to an col aquillos polvos

Pye crust, take it out by Parcels & make thereof (vpon
a sheete of ^cleane paper with a  little sugar sprinkeled thereon for
clinging)  a little coule & cut it into little peeces, of which
(between your fingers) you are to make the Pastillas;
which being made, must be layd on a sheet of clean paper
(note that as one is making the the Pastillas, another be 6
pounding & turning the paste; & if it shall at any tyme
happen grow somwhat hard, you may put therein a few
drops of sweet water) & soe put them where they may
drye & grow hard which willbe in 2 or 3 dayes.

Pomos de Olor para la
Lumbre of Iohn Moreno

Put the Pomo ouer the Fire, putting therein a good
quantity of sweet water; of ambar is the best but a any will
serue; when it is warme put therein soe much Beniamin
(which they call heer Menjuy) pounded as will fill the
Pomo somewhat more then halfe full; then take a quarter
of an ounce of storax, & pound it & put it into the Pomo
an ochaua of the best cloues & doe the like,; an ounce of
Poluillo de Mosqueta, half an ounce of ambargrees (black
may serue the turne) a quarter of an ounce of  Ciuet; these 96
are to be pounded euery thing by it selfe; & to be put
into the Pomo as it stands ouer the fire, you may
put therein some slices of the outermost rind of a lemmon ,
& heere they vse to put some slices of the skin of an
aple called the Camueça. You must obserue that the
Pomo must be put ouer a gentle fire; & aboue all that
it be neuer sent to the fire without good store of perfect
sweet water; for if it be put dry to the fire it will burne
& cling to the sides of the Pomo & consume presently,
but with water it will last many yeares.

Note that all all these ingredients must haue
their consideration according to the bignes of the Pomo
This is made with 12 ounces of Beniamin, according to
which proportion, you may gouern your selfs.

Note that the  ambar & ciuet must be put in 8
when the Beniamin is melted in the water, but the
water must be poured out, & the ambar & ciuet put
into the Beniamin, & there be suffred to incorporate
gently. Last of all must be put in the lemmon Pill;
& then returne the same water.

To make Pastillas





97 42
To make Pastillas de fuego of
Iohn Moreno's fashion

Because Benjamin is the substance thereof you must note that
it must be prepared, which is in the manner following: Take
Benjamin, & pound it small & sift it; then put it into an
earthen vessell, that is well glased within, & put thereto
so much Rose water, as shall plentifully couer it; & with
that proportion of water, you must allwayes keepe itt.
Then put it into ?? ??  the sunne in the heat of the yeare;
& there let it be as long as you can; for the more sunne it
hath the better; but 15 dayes is the least (according to the
heat of Spaine) This is the best manner of preparing and
therefore it is good to prepare in the sumer for all the yeare,
8 But in a necessity, they vse to put the Benjamin, as abouesaid,
into a Kettle; & with some Rose water let it dissolue
& then let it repose. Being thus prepared when you will
make your Pastilles; Take a pound thereof (or more if you
list but euer by weight) & put in a Kettle with some rose=
water & lett it soften, vntill it ^may be wrought  with the finger;
& then take out the water, & power therein a quarter of
an ounce of ambar, & 2 adarmes of ciuet (the adarme is
the 16th part of an ounce) which might be done in this
manner: Take a brass mortar & put it ouer the coales,
vntill it be soe warme as it will melt the ambar, which
being done (stirring it with the Pestle) you may put thereto
your ciuet; and all being melted together put it to the
paste of Benjamin (the water being out) and with
98 your Pestle worke it alltogether. Then returne to put
thereto the water you tooke from thence; & let it
stand ouer the fire, to keepe it in such a temper, as it
may be wrought into Pastillas, & noe otherwise. The
proportions heerein mentioned make soe good, as any
King need vse; yet you may adde thereto as you please.
& soe may you take from it./

Aqua Mirabilis
(1) Take Galingall, Cloves, quibibs, ginger, melitot, cardamome,
mace, nutmeggs, of each a dram, and if the Iuice of
Caelandine halfe a pinte, the Iuice of mints and balme
together halfe a pinte, and mingle all the said spices,
being beaten in powder, with the Iuice, and with a pinte
of good Aquauite, and 3 pintes of good white wine, and
put all these together into a pott and let it stand soe
all night, being close stopt, and on the morrow still it
with as soft a fire as can be, the still being close pasted
this water is of a secret nature, it dissolued the swelling
of the lunges without any grieuance, and the lunges being
wounded and perished it mightily helpeth and cherisheth
them, and suffereth not the bloode to putrifie, he shall
neuer need to be lett blood that vseth this water it suffereth
not the heart to be brent nor yet melencholy or fleagme
to be lifted vp or to haue dominion aboue Natuer : it allso
expelleth the Rheume and profiteth the stomack and
preserueth ^ youth in its one state it ingendireth a good culler keep keepeth & preserueth, the visage and memory, of all the waters
artificiall it is the best, and in the summer vse once a
weeke fasting the quantity of one spoone full, & in the
winter two a weeke;

2 To make the water
Imperiall
Take scabious, pimpernell, Betonie, of each foure handsfull,
Dragons burnets with the knobbs, foure handsfull, tormentill
with rootes also . 4 . handsfull, ditander . 3. handsfull, lay all
these vpon a board . 3 dayes then shred them together small
then steepe them in a gallon of white wine and soe let it
stand . 24 . houres close stopt, then distill it in a cold still,
then take the water and put to it two ounces of mythridate,
or good Treacle of venice, three penny worth of Turmerick, one
pound of suggar, fouer sticks of licorish, one handfull of
anisseed halfe a peck of Borrage or Bugclosse flowers and
soe distill it againe





3A Restoratiue made of
Rosasolis, to be gathered
in June and July
I warne you in any wise when you gather this flowers of
Rosasolis, that you touch not the herbe it selfe, for then
the vertue of it is gone, you must pluck it vp by the -
stalkes out of the ground, and lay it in a basket, Take
as much Rosasolis as will fill a pottle pott, and wash it
not in any wise, take a pottle of good Aquauite, and put
them both out into a vessell and let it stand hard stopt
for three dayes and three nights, and the fourthe daye
straine it through a cleane linnen cloth, into a glasse
or peuter pott, and put thereto a pound of suggar beaten
small, halfe a pound of licorish beaten into fine powder,
halfe a pounde of Dates and take out the states, and cutt
them in small peeces, mingle all these together and stop
the glasse or pott very close that noe ayre come to it, and
drinke of that to beadward halfe a spoone full of ale and
soe in the morning fasting

4To make Doctor Stephens
water
Take a gallon of Claret wine, a handfull of Rosemary,
a handfull of lauender, a handfull of Camamile, a -
handfull of Red Sage, a handfull of pellitory of the wall,
of wilde time, of garden time, of mints, of each a handfull,
two handfulls of red rose leaves, anisseeds, carraway
seeds, fennell seeds, ginger cloues, cinamon, nutmeggs,
galingall roots, graines, of each bruise all
the spice and seeds, seuerally, also bruise the herbes, and
steepe them alltogether in the claret wine, 24. houres -
and distill them with a sofft fier in a limbeck, keepe
the first pinte by it selfe, a little more will serue
to drinke the rest for washing water.

5To make an Excellent
water for the eyes onely
Take of Eye bright, of three leaued grasse with the specks
in it, of daysie leaves and rootes, of ground Iuice, of broun
fennell, of hearbe grasse, of wormwood of salendine





52
Crootsroots and all, of pimpernell, of each of these one
handfull, take all these and put them into an -
ordinary still, and with them a good handfull of
redrose leaves and an other of white, and after -
the water is distiled take a little of the purest
white Coper rose, and a little white sugger candy
put them in the water, and Let it stand in the sunne
for a time to clarifie, these herebs also if they be
put into a pott & put theseto a potte of water, and
stand macerating ouer warme embers . 2 . or 3 . nights
and stand and then let them steepe tenne or twelve
dayes, and then take the hearbes away from the water,
and put them c..iris to it and suggar candy, it is uery
good that way, but better stilled, .

6 A rare and Excellent water to
preserue one from the Plague in
time of infection, and if he be
infected to expell and driue the
same from the principall parts of
the heart and the head without
any danger, practised in London
by a ffrench man to his great gaine
and many a mans safety, haueing
had it taught me by one that was
his man
Take of the hearbe dragon, of Adders tongue of Agrimony
of scabious, of salendine, of pimpernell, or sage of Rosema ^ ry
of Rew, of wormwood of muggworth of baulme of each
a pound, lay them steeping in as much white wine as
will couer them, a day and a night, then still them
and drinke the distilled water first in the morning
and also afterwards in the day time as you shall thinke
good





7The right and perfect way
of distilling all sorts of waters
simple or compounded, with
their oyles and how to separate
the one from the other

Take of your best anisseed  . 2 pound being somewhat
bruised then adde thereunto of the best whit wine . 2 .
quarts, of muske in a peece of sarcenett to the value
of . 4 . grains, to be distilled with this water putting
all into the vessell, pasting the limbeck very close,
then paste your receiuer that no steeme or fume come
forth then with a soft and carefull fier, and in the
name of God still it, and from this potte you shall
haue a quart with an ounce of your oyle of anisseed
which will be when your water is forth within an houre,
separate the one from the other for it will congeale
to the liknesse of a peece of snow, this is most precious
to expell winde, and the water for e winde collick .

8To distill all sorts of hearbes
& to draw their oyle withall
Take fennell and cut it somewhat smale to the value
of halfe a peck and sett it in the vessell, then adde -
thereto of running water the purest you can gett
and fill the vessel therewith then lett ouer your
limbeck with past theron next your receiuer, that
no fume come forth thereat then with a souft fire
distill it, and there will come both water, & oyle, then
to separate the oyle from the water with a siluer
spoone take it from the top and reserue it for a precious
medicine against the menstru or termes, the water
will haue the smell taste and tincture of the fennell
and it is very good against sore eyes and to coole
feauers





53
9To make Cinamon water after the best manner

Take a gallon and a halfe of the best sack you can
gett or els the best and strongest madera wine and
a pinte of redrose water or halfe a pinte of damaske
rose water, put a pound of Cinamon broken into your
wine, into a pott very clos stopped, let it steepe soe .2.
nights and a day, then distill forth into your lymbeck
beeing close stopped, with a very souft fire, and keepe
the very first comming of it by it selfe, & soe let the
second and third, and this is the perfect making
of Cinamon water.

10To make Baulme water

Take 4 Gallons of the strongest Ale you can make,
5- or 6- handsfull of baulme anisseed three quarters
of a pound well bruised of licorish halfe a pound well
scraped and bruised, of ginger a quarter of a pound
and grossely Beaten, of fennell seed a quarter of a
pound grossely beaten of Alysanders grossely beaten
one ounce of Carrawaye seeds grossly beaten - 2. ounces
steepe all these together in the Ale in a vessel close
stopped 2. nights and 2 dayes then distill it, your
lymbeck being very close stopped with a souft fier
at the first & towards the latter ending the fire
somewhat quicker keepe each drawning by it selfe
11 Memorandum that after the order above said, you may make
all sorts of water as of Angelica spearemint wormewood,
or any other hearbes adding thereunto raisons of the
sunne stoned and figgs finely sliced if you please,
also if you will make Aquavite adde those hearbes to the
seeds and liquors above written wormewood, southern
wood spearmint, time, baulme, pennyriall, hysop, rosemary
tops and blossoms of each a good handfull, cut or
bruised.







12Cardus Benedictus water, vsually
called the holy Thistle
The proprotion afore named of seeds and ale, and a
pound of Cardus and betany together, to euery gallon
of Ale with raisons of the sunne stoned, and ginger -
and figgs sliced, makes an excellent comfortable
water

13To make Bezoar water, which
is called the water of life

Take good greeke wine or sack of the best you can gett
and pleasant, distill it in a lymbeck, or other still
3 times and after in a glasse still twice, which being
done, it will come to the spirit of wine which you must
preserue to your vse in thick glasses, of many gallons
you shall haue but a small quantity, being soe often
drawne, then take damaske rose water a quart, or a
pottle, and put into it great treacle of venice, a pretty
quantity, & still it in a glasse still, and keepe it close
and when you will make the water, take as much rose
water, as of the spirit of wine, put into euery pinte
half a pounde of white sugger candy fine beaten then
take a little quantity of Ambergris, muske Bezer
stone, white saunderes, and let them boyle in the
liquor and soe keepe it and when you will use it strain
it
14A water for the yellow Jaundis
And the stone
Take 12 gallons of as strong ale as you can gett, and a
peck of garden snailes 4 . or . 5 . ounces of harts horne
and as much cloues, you must take your snailes and
roaste them in charcole fire letting them lye till they
hisse or make a noise, then take them out and wipe them
and the shell also very cleane with a linnen cloth, beate
them in a morter, and lay them to steep in your ale
all night, likwise you must take 2 . handsfull of salendine
which you must put in the morning following into
your lymbeck with the harts horne and cloues above named





54
before you put these things into your lymbeck
brake your salendine betweene your hands and
strewe it in the bottom of your pot with half a hand
full of Angelica, and tso power on them all the other
things and still them with a sofft fire keeping the
first water by it selfe, when you put in those things,
you may put in a pint more of Ale, if you thinke good
take the first morning 4 . spoones full of it and . 3 .
of Ale, and the next morning . 3. of it and . 4. of Ale,
and the third . 2 spoones full and 3 . of Ale then take
it noe more till 3 . or 4 mornings be past

15A precious water to recouer
the Consumption
Take an old red cocke and hold the beake or bill
hard in your fingers till he be smothered, then
pull him quickly before he be cold head, and all,
and slit the cocke from the head all along the back
bone, through out, and quarter it and bruise the bones
and put it into a close earthen pot, well stopped
and put into a pot of water, ouer the fier . 3 or . 4 houres
then take it off and straine all the Juice out and
keepe it and put to the Cock a large mace and a
little rosewater, and what you thinke fitt to rellish
the pallate and stoppe the pot close, and stirre it
againe till all the substance be out then take all
the Juice out and put it to the other you had before
seasoning it with comfortable things, and give 4 .
spoones full in the morning, soe in the afternoone
and at night in the last stewing you must put in
white sugger candy and . 3 . little peeces of Cinamon

16A souueraigne water of an Ancient
Doctor of Physick, which he long
used and therewith did many
cures, he kept it very secret to
himselfe till he died, and then
he gaue it in writing to an Arch
bishop of Canterbury

Take a Gallon of good Gascon Claret wine, ginger, cinamon,
nutmeggs, graines, cloues, anisseed, fennell seeds, Carraway
seeds of each a dram, of sage, red mint, roses time





Pellitory, rosemary, wild time, Cammomill, lauender,
of each one handfull, then bray the spices, and bray the
hearbes, and put them into the wine, and let it stand
soe . 12 . houres stirring it often, then still it together
in a lymbeck, keepe the first water by it selfe and
the second by it selfe, for it is good though not soe good as the first

The vertue of this water is to comfort the stomack
and vitall parts it helpeth inward diseases that
come of cold, and the shaking palsey, it cureth the
contraction of sinnewes, helpeth the conception of
woman, it killeth wormes in the belly, cureth the cold
cough, helpeth the tooth Ache, cureth cold dropsies
dissolued stones in the bladder, and ?aimes, helpeth
shortly a stinking breath, and who useth it some times
and not too often, it preserueth in good liking, and
maketh him looke younge very long, comforteth nature
maruellous long, the old Docter herewith preserued
his life till extreame old age would neither suffer
him to goe or stand and after Physitians had giuen
him ouer, he continued fiue yeares, when they thought
he could not live two yeares, and before his death he
confessed, that when he was sicke at any time, he
used nothing but this water, the Archbishop used it
til he was 1010 yeares old, and said at his death
that nothing did him soe much good, as this water in
Sicknesse or otherwise M Rowe Sergeant of the Coife
used it all his time, and at his death left it in
writing wishing all men to use it
If it be sett in the sunne all the sommer it will be
the better

17 A water for the stone in
the Kidneys
Take a good handfull of pellitory of the wall saxifrage
garden parsley and wild time of each one handfull
3 . spoonsfull of fennel seed and three radish rootes
sliced, shred all these together and put them into
a gallon of new milke and let them stand and steepe
in a close pot one whole night then still them





55
This must be done in the latter end of June or
the beginning of July, for then the hearbes are
in theire best taste, you must put . 3 . or . 4 . spoons
full of this into as much white wine, with a little
sliced nutmegg, and soe take it two dayes before
the change of the moone, and 2 dayes after &
two dayes before and after the full, continuing
to take it soe all the yeare, and patient shall
undoubtedly finde great ease, voiding many
stones and much grauell without any paines at all,

18A water for wasting or Consumption
of the Kidneys

Take a gallon of new milke, and still it in a cold
still, but draw but one pinte of water out of it,
drinke of this in the morning, in the afternoone
and all night, and it will cure you,

19An other for the same

20A water for fainting

Take and still Isop, mints and rosemary a like
quantity of each, keepe the water thereof for that
use to drinke when need it

21A water for wasting of the
stone in the Kidneys

Take pennyriall and boile it in whit wine & drink
it, it prouoketh urine and also breaketh the stone,





22A water for a bruise or a vaine
broken in the bellie

potione 5Take sunacle, selfe heale, yarrow, comfrey, wrootes and hysop,
saniele of all a like quantitie, and soe still it in an ordinary
still, and giue it the patient to drinke .4. or .5. spoones
full all a time, with as much burnt claret wine
or posset drinke.

23An Excellent moauth water to kill
any strong Canker or worme

Take Costmary, rosemary, sauin, hearbgrace, redd sage
wormwood, Camomill, mallowes, muggworth, mercury
salendine, of each a good handfull, but most of womwood
a gallon or better of faire running water, boyle it ouer
a sofft fire, till the leaves be tender, and the liquor
become to the quantity of a quart, then put in a peece
of Allom as bigg as a pullets egg, & 3 good spoonesfull
of English honey, boyle this a whalme or two till the
Allome be melted and the honey incorporated into the
water, then put up your water and hearbes to your use

24An Excellent Syrop for the opening
of the Lunges and stopping of the
stomack and cleansing of the blood
and to cure consumption

Take a handfull of wormwood, one handfull of hearbegreace
a little time, halfe a handfull of organ, and soe of
marierom, one handfull of liuerworth, a little mugworth
a handfull of hysop, one handfull of Agrymonie, 3. or . 4 .
roots of parsely and soe of fennell rootes the pith taken
out of the midle of the roots, & a handfull of coltsfoot,
a pretty quantity of Elicampane roots them sliced, a
little sweet fennell seeds and asmuch aniseede, carraway
seeds and Coriander seed . one handfull of maidenhaire
as much of sydrake 8 . or . 9 . dates and as many figgs
2 handsfull of the best blew raisins of the sunne and
a pretty quantity of English licorish, a race or two of
ginger, cut in slices, take all these and put them into
a close earthen pott, put to them . 3. pintes or a pottle of
the strongest and stalest beere you can get, stop the
pott close and paste it round that no ayre come to it
put it into the ouen with your batch and let it stewe
there all the while your bread is in the ouen, and when you draw
your






56
your pot must stand to soake your hearbes Iuice into the licor,
then take the pot out of the ouen and straine it through
a cloth into a skillet very cleane all the Iuice of the hearbes
then put it ouer the fier, and put to it a pound of suger
candy and neare . 2 . pound of the best English honey &
soe boyle it to a syrop, scum it and then keepe it in a
close glasse, and lett the pary troubled take a spoone
full at night, and a nother in the morning when they
awake, or at any other time, when your syrop is boyled
enough put in as much saffron as will lye uppon a
3pence, very finely dried and bruised to a fine powder
soe giue it a whalme

25A Syrop for the shortnes of

Take . 3 . or . 4 . young fennell roots . 2 . roots of succory
4 roots of                 make them clean and pick out
the pith of them and cut them an inch long, then
put them into a quart of faire runing water, and
set them ouer the fier, and put thereto .4. crops of
hore hound . 4 . or . 5 crops of                   a quantitie of
the flowers of Isop or if there be noe flowers take
the leaves, also a quantity of penniriall, well washed
let them boyle till you see the colour of the hearbes
but let the rootes boile till you thinke the licor be
halfe boyled away and more, then take it of from
the fier straine the licor from the roots, and make
your vessel cleane, and put it on the fier againe,
then put to it six ounces of suger candy and a stick
of Cinnamon soe long as your finger breaking
it in peeces and soe let it seeth till it come to
a Syrop, and take thereof morning and euening

26To make syrop of violetts blue

Take of garden violets . 6 . ounces, more or lesse beat
them in a stone morter and take the Iuice thereof, put
it into a gelly pot of a pound sett the pott in a skillet of
water, that the Iuice may boile, and when it begins to growe
thick, put in 8 ounces of suger soe boyle it quick to a syrop
this syrop is good for the tissick to open the pipes





27To make a syrop of Centory, to
Expell Poyson from the heart
cleanse the blood, and is good for
diuers infirmities as the wormes &c

Take Centory when it is in the prime of greenesse, and distill
it in an ordinary still, take a gallon or . 3 quarts of water,
and keepe it till you gather the other Centory in its due
time, which will be in haruest betweene the two Lady dayes
soe if you distill the water, you may make the syrop at any
time of the winter.
Take a good quantity of Centory, Cut it very small, stalkes seeds
and all, keepe this Centory tenn dayes in the distilled water
and then boile it very well in a cleane skillet, then take it
of and straine it through a cleane cloath into an other
cleane skillet, then put in . 2 . or 3 . fennel mores and soe of
parseley mores and smallage mores, boile the mores and cast
away the pith of them then put into the same liquor a quart
of the purest English honey, one pound of the whitest sugar
Candy, and a pound of the best loafe suger, bile it gentle ouer
a sofft fire, skimming it as you see occasion and when it is
allmost come to a syrop, make a quick fire under it and
boill it vp to a syrop and put it vp in glasses, and
when you haue occasion to use it, take euery morning
a spoonefull in what stilled water you thinke fittest
for . 5 . mornings together, fasting one houre after, if
you can get no water of Centory, which is the purer
way, then take faire runing water, and steepe a
greater quantity of centory in the same quantity
of water . 10 . or 14 dayes, and when you haue halfe
boiled it take out your centory and bouilebruseit very
well, and put it into the liquor againe, that all the
Iuice of the Centory may boile out of it soe make
your syrop as aboue written.

28To make the syrop of Elicumpane
which is good for all the Inward
parts of the body, and against
extreame colds and to breake the
fleagmes for the stopping of
the stomake and against the
Consumption

Take a handfull of Celerach, one of hore hound soe of
maidenhaire soe of liuerworth, Coltsfoot half a handfull
a good handfull of pimpernell, 2. or .3. spriggs of wormwood
soe of hearbegrace half a handfull of marierom, soe of
time, Isop comomill flowers, burrage leaues and flowers
rosemary topps and flowers one handfull, soe of betony
one or two fennell roots, soe of perseley rootes, but cast
castaway





57
away the pith of them, and slice the rootes, take Elicampane
rootes, thinly sliced, a little handfull of aniseeds bruised
2 ounces, and 2 . ounces of licorish thinly sliced, 3 or 4 races
of giners, allmost a pound of raisins of the sunne halfe
a pound of figgs, soe of dates, slice them thinne, steepe
all these in . 3 quarts or a gallon of the strongest and

29For the spleene

Take oyle of capers and half as much of oyle of vineger
of squille, melt them together and annoint the vaine of
the spleene warme, and dip clothes therein and crouch
it out gently and lay it to the spleene and continue
it all day. Also take the leaves of tamariske and bruse
not aboue one on two stalkes in ale or beare and
drinke thereof euery morning and euening strained

30An Excellent powder for the cough
of the lunges & Consumption

Take an ounce of foxe lunges, one ounce of Elicompane
first washed in whit wine, cut in round slices, then
put uppon a threed and dried then beaten to powder
a quantity of licorish and soe of anisseeds, as much
of ginger as of fox lunges and soe of suger candy beate
all these to a fine powder, mingel them together & at night
and at morning, take as much as will lye uppon a six pence
at any time when you feele your selfe troubled





31ffor the Cough of the Lungs
Take a gallon of faire running water, a handfull of
figgs, as much Coltsfoot, the like of maidenhaire a
handful of succory, as much of Endive, the like
quantity of perseley roots and 8 dates, boile all these
together till it come to 3 pintes, then straine the water
from them and boile a pound of suger with the said
water and put to it . 3 . or . 4 spoonsfull of vineger and
soe use it

32ffor a Consumption or burning
feauer

Take 40. or 50. house snailes, and pick them out of the
shell, scower them with water first, then once or twice
with white wine, drie them in a cleane cloth, and put
them into a pottle of milke of a cowe of one colour
and put it into a flagon or earthen pot & soe boile
it in a kettle of water on the fier, till it comes to
three pintes, or lesse, drinke of this morning and euening,
with a quantity of conserue of red roses, and as much
flowers of brimstone as will lye uppon a three pence, in
each draught if it be a feauer allone leaue out the
flowere of Brimstone

33Docter Burges his medicine
for the plague
Take .3 pintes of malmsey and boile therin one handfull
of sage, as much hRue, till a pinte be wasted, then straine it
and set it on the fire againe and put thereto a pennyworth
of long pepper, halfe an ounce of ginger, a quarter of an
ounce of nutmeggs, all beaten together, then let it boyle a
little and put thereto an ounce of mythridate, halfe an ounce
of Treacle, a quarter of a pinte of good Aquauitae, or hot
Angelica water, which is better, keepe this as your life aboue
all wordly treasure take it alwayes warme both morning and
euining a spoonefull or two if you be allreadie diseased sweat
thereupon, if not once a day a spoonefull is sufficient, in all
the plauge time, under God trust to this for certaine, for
there was neuer man, woman, nor childe, that this deceiued
if the hart be not quiet mortified & drowned with this
disease before, this is not onely good for the common
plague which is called the sicknes, but also for the small
pox measles, surfets, Agues and diuers other kindes of
diseases





58
34A fume for the head

Take Rosemary, sage and wormewood of each a good handfull
boile them in a pottle of redeowes milke till it come to a quart
then put in a handfull of Cummin seeds, bruised and stirre
it about, put it into a narrow mouthed pot, and hold the mouth
of the pot close to your Eare, an hower at night when you goe
to bead, use it 6 . or 7 nights together, the same liquor will serue

35An approued Medicine for
an Ague

Take a spoonefull of Baysalt, 2 spoonesfull of Sooth, one hand
full of wallflower leaues, beate the salt uery small so like
wise your leaves, then temper all these together with the
yolke of an egge, till it be like a salue, then lay it to the
patients wrest onely within the inside where the veines
lye, this must be applied euery night to his wrest while
the Ague lasteth

36For an Extreame hott Ague,
take this Drinke

Take the Juice of 6 . orenges and a little milke and make
a posset of the Juice, straine it & put in some fine sugar
soe much as the patient may drinke it, if the ague take
him in the morning this must be taken ouer night, the
last thing when he goeth to bed, if it takes him towards
the afternoone it must be drunke the first thing in the
morning, it is uery good

37A Medicine for the Joint Ague

Take . 2 . quarts of strong Ale, and 2 . good handsfull of
Ribworth boile these together untill it be boiled halfe then
straine it from the hearbes, and put to the liquor one good
spoonesfull of Bolearmonack finely beaten into powder and
4 spoonsfull of treacle of Jane or mythridatum, mingle
well all thes together, and halfe an hower before the fit
commeth take . 9 spoonsfull of the drinke being warmed &
within a quarter of an houre take 7 spoonsfull more and
at the end of the house after this drinke third part of the
houre . 5 spoonsfull, and at the end of the houre 3 spoonesfull
sweate an houre after this drinke taken, and then take some
warme broath, but beware of cold drinke, and soe in like manner
as before use the medicine untill it be all done, & take heaed
of cold all the time of drinking this medicine





38An approued medicine for the
bloudy flux

Take a pinte of red wheate and dust it uery well, and beate it
in a morter, and dust it no more, let it be but Grentelled, &
soe boyle it in new milke, as you doe use to doe in water, you
must allow a gallon or . 5 quarts of milke to a poundpint of
wheate or there abouts, and boyle it till it ...... to the thicknes
of a ferment, and when it is well boiled, put therein one
good sponefull of powder of pome granat pill or shell
and as much of Cinamon, stirre it well together and
eate it as your stomack will serue you and this I hope
shall helpe you

39A uery good Medicine for the
Laske or Bloudy flux

Take Red wine in one pott, and new milke in an other, then
take two stiff strawes, and put one in the said milke, &
the other in the wine, cut them of an euen length, and
put the other end of them in your mouth, suck of the wine
and milke in this manner soe often as you shall thinke
good, untill you finde to Begin to staye, and then drink
not soe often as before for feare of staying it too soudenly

40 For the Lask

Take a pinte of malmesey, halfe a pinte of red rose
water, and a quarter of a pint of Cardus water, a
nutmegg cut in quarters and a large mace, boyle
all these together, and when they are well beaten
mingle them with the foresaid stuffe, and make
thereof a Cawdle & when it is taken off the fire, sweeten
it with as much suger as will please the tast &
drinke thereof a good draught morning & euening

41For the staying of the
Loosenesse
Take the shell of an Acorne dried and Cinamon &
pome granat pill of each a little quantity beat all
these together uery small and take of that powder
a spoonfull a put it into a draught of redwine
and drinke it





59
42To stay a uomiting or Loosenes

Take a pinte of Malmsey, halfe a pinte of redrose water
a quarter of a pinte of Cardus water a nutmegg, a
large mace boile all these together ouer a soft fire
then beate 2 eggs uery well with the white and all
and dresse it as you doe an other Cawdle, and giue
it the party grieued, a quarter of a pinte at a time
cold or hot as the patient like it,

.3A Remedy for the Collick and
Stone

Take one handfull of Hawes and pound them to powder
and 4 bay berries a quart of whit wine boile all these
together till the third part be boiled away then
straine it and sweeten it with suger

44For the stone and winde
Collick

46Against the wind though it

Take one ounce of Cinamon water, one ounce of

47For the stone a uery good
receipt

The flowers of Elder, the shells of Eggs that haue hatcht
chickens, newly after the henne hath hatched them, and
drie them soe you must the Elder flowers, then beate
them seuerally, and take halfe an ounce of the Elder
powder ^ . a quarter of an ounce of the pouder of the Eggshells, and of powder of pearles as much
as of the Eggshells mingele these together, and keepe
them close in a paper bagge, and soe giue of that as
much in quantity as will lye uppon a six pence
at a time in white wine, with sugger and nutmegg,
or in broth or posset, morning and euening for
5 . or 7 dayes, when you are most pained, or in the
weane of the moone, and as you finde your selfe
better by the taking of it, use it the oftener

48A Drinke for the winde of the
stomake and spleene

Take a handfull of mother of time, which is the large
time, bruise it a little in wringing it betweene your
hands, steepe it all night in a draught of whit wine
well sweetened with suger in a siluer pott, and couer
it, and put thereto . 5. or 6. slices of ginger, and in the
morning fasting drinke a draught thereof, and
walke after it halfe an houre and more take this
drinke . 4 . or 5 . mornings together, and twice or thrice
a weeke you must take your purgation broth of
polipody

49To purge Malancoly
Take Aloes the purest in powder, diagridion of each
of them the weight of fower pence put it in conserue
of roses thi weight of six pence, take it in the
morning fasting and abstaine from meate . 2. howers,
then take a little warme broth, keeping the Chamver
for that day

50An other for the same

Take Rhubarbe the purest the weight of a shilling
in fine powder with Conserue of roses the weight
of six pence .

51A gainst Malancoly

Take wallflowers . 6 . handfull, Rosemary flowers
6 handsfull, and as much of damask rose leaues
of Burrage and buglos flowers . 4 handsfull, of
Baulme leaues . 22 handfull, of Cinamon grossly
beaten . 2 ounces, 4 nutmeggs, 2 ounces of aniseeds
grossely beaten, of English saffron the weight of
six pence of pinke flowers . 6 . handsfull, putt all
these into two gealleons of sack and let it stand
soe . 2 dayes, now and then turning them afterwards
distill them in a faire lymbeck uery clossly pasted
hauing 4 grames of muske in a fine cloth, that the
distilled licor may runne through it into the glasse
that standeth under the nose of the still to receiue
the water you shall put 12 ounces of whit suggar
Candy beaten to powder to it, that it may incorporate
with the said liquor when all is distiled then let
the glasse that conteineth the water in hot water for
an houre that the suggar may be incorporated with it
of this take 3 . times in a weeke fasting . 2 . poones
full at a time also if you finde your selfe heauie
spirited lett your fire be as sofft as may be, else
will the drinke be the lesse.

52The manner how to make your
purgation broth which purgeth
malancoly fleagme, it easeth
the Colick, hepeth the winde
& cureth the spleene

Take polypody of the oake, or the ash which is better
pick it cleane and bruise it, put it in water & oatmeale
as you were wont to doe or if you put to it as much
whit wine as water, and 3 . or 4 good slices of ginger
and a few small raisins, and when your broth is boiled
straine it and put to a draught the ..of 2 or 3 spoons
full of syrop of roses and drinke it fasting, and wth in
halfe an houre after walke to make it worke the better, this
will giue you . 3 or 4 . stooles at a time if your body be easily
moued, this purgation is uery gentle, it may be taken att
all times, when you feare to take Glysters or other strong
purgations which could be dangerous in a weake body, wth out
the aduice of some Physition





53

54For a Burning

Take the rinde of an Elder tree, seeth it halfe a day
in faire water, then let it coole and gather the thick
of the water with a feather and euery day with a
feather oint the sore euening and morning

55For a kinde of Burning with
Gunpowder or other wise

Take the whites of Eggs and beate them till they foame then
scume it off cleane, and put to the whites of Eggs as much
oyle of Roses and beate them together, and oint the burning
therewith, and if it be not on the face, lay woodbine leaues
or bay leaues thereon

56An other for a Burning

Take snow water and the white of Eggss & the Juice of
house leeke, of all a like quantity, and beat them together
till they come to an oyle, and then dresse the sore with
it twice or thrice a day, and it will helpe it, making it
fresh now and then lay this cooling seare cloth following uppon it,
and though it be grieuiously burned yet it will helpe it

57For a scalding

Take fresh Goose dung and frye it with butter, and sheeps
suet, straine it through a fine cloth, and lay it one the
sore, and it will helpe

58Against the Pleuresie

Take the rootes of nettles cleane washed and seeth
them in milke, or for want of that in drinke, then
putt pepper to it, and drinke it hott, and it will helpe
you





61
59To brake the fleagmes and
Comfort the Stomack

Take a quart of stale strong beere, and boile in it a hand
full of organ, of spearemint . 2 . or 3 branches, Isop 5 .
branches, licorish one ounce scraped and sliced . 6 . figgs
cut in peeces, halfe an ounce of anisseeds, raisins of the
sunne . halfe a handfull pickt and stoned, a large mace
boile all these together till it come to a pinte ouer a gentle
fire, then straine it and boile with hony loafe sugger
till it come to a syrop, giue of it 2 . or . 3 spoonsfull
at a time

60An Excellent Drinke to be taken
of those, that fetch their breath
like sighes, with heuines of
the hart

Take . 2 . grains of muske, as much of saffron as will lye
upon . 3 pence, uery well dried and finely bruised, and
suger, these three finely bruised together, then mixe
them well with an ounce of Conserue of Damaske roses
4. or . 5 spoonsfull of Damaske rose water, and as much
Claret wine, and if you haue noe Claret wine, take white
wine, and warme this and drinke it in the morning
fasting, and sleepe an houre upon the left side, & fast
an houre more, and it shall giue  your spirirts a great
reuiuing

61The Note of the Scuruygrasse
drinke

Take sassafrasse thin sliced, fennell rootes, parseley
rootes of each . 2 . or . 3 ounces, of the barke of Tamariske
one ounce and a halfe, of the inner rindes of the Ash of
Elder, of each one ounce of Ashen heyes 2 handsfull all
these ayred, and beaten to a gross powder, with anisseeds
and Grommell seed bruised, of each . 2 . spoonesfull, keepe
these powders to hange in a uessel of Drinke made as
followeth also of nutmeggs an ounce

Take scuruy grasse well picked 6 . handsfull, of Brooke lime
and water Cresses of each . 2 handsfull, of sweete pippins
sliced . 20 in number, boile these hearbes and apples with
a few hoppes in 4 hallons of midling worth, till allmost
halfe be consumed, then worke it up, being strained from
the hearbes with barme, and when it is ripe, tunne it up into your
uessell wherein your bagge is, with a lead or stone to keepe it downe
in





in . 6 . dayes after you may drinke thereof, first in the
morning, and att 4 . in the afternoone and late at night
and that what quantity you please

62A Medicine for the scuruy or for
an Ache in the bones

Take the wood of sassafras cut into thin slices of the
thicknes of 3 pence or a groate, the weight of 6. drammes
or of 3 . shillings . 4 pence, of licorish bruised halfe an
ounce french barly . 2 spoonsfull, agrimony and spoone wort
called garden scuruy grasse or Cochlearia, and of water
Cresses, and water pursline or brookelime, of each halfe
a handfull bruised, of aniseeds a spoonefull seeth all
these in 3 quarter of faire water till pBut one pottle
remaine, straine it and let it setle in an earthen panne
till it be cold, then powre the clearest thereof into a glasse
uiall . drinke a good draught of this euery morning . 3 .
houres before dinner, and likewise an other att 3 a clock
in the afternoone, continue this . 14 or 20 dayes, the pot
wherein you seeth this things, must be close stopped

63A Bath for the same disease

Take halfe a peck of Elder buds, and as much of ground
Iuie, primroses, Isop, featherfew Camomill and nettles
a lapp full together all being a bushell, seeth them
in . 4 . gallons of runing water, and 2 gallons of wine leese
& a pint of wine vinager & put them into a great tub & lay a block in it set ^
your feet on the block, and wrappe you warme that noe
ayre come to you, and annoint you first with this
oyntment naked, whereof the receipt hereafter doth
follow, and wrappe you in a sheete and goe into the
tubbe, then couer you ouer with a couerled, that you
take no cold or ayre, but onely to breath, use this . 4
dayes together and sweat 2 houres euery day. the
same licor will serue uery well if it be throughly
boiled euery day when you use it.

64A Drinke for those that haue
the kinges euill

The name of the herbe is called redseges and it is like
sampier, but it is red. this herbe must be drunke . 2. or three
dayes both morning and euening you may drinke this drinke
a yeare euery time when the moone is in the new, or from
the spring till midsummer be past, and euery good fryday
soe long as the party is grieued, you must drinke this
on that daye, and 3 dayes after, at what time the moone
serueth best, you must take the quantity of a handfull
and pick it cleane and wash it, shred it on a trencher and
pound it uery small, & put it into a pinte of beere or ale
in a stone Jugge and set it in the Celler, and let the
party drinke it as it is before prescribed, you must gather
this hearbe att the begining of Aprill, And keep it dry to
use in winter as is aforesaid, you must gather that which
growth upon walls and beareth a white flower, for that
is best

65For the obstructions of
the Liuer

Take of fennell parseley and succory rootes of each
two rootes, scrape them and take the pitch out, then take
polypodium and grasse rootes of each . 2 ounces, boile the
rootes in . 4 pintes of water untol . 3 . then adde of Endiue
succory, scabious, uiolets hartes tongue, maidenhaire
burrage, and buglosse of each half a handfull
2 ounces boile these a while on the fire
then infuse of one ounce, anisseeds and
corinader of each 2 drames, infuse these all night
then straine them in the morning and adde to
tbhis decoction 4 ounces of salendine, let him drinke
twice a day halfe a pinte at a draight






66A Receipt for lack of nature
and to restore it againe

Take a peck of snailes that be in gardens, hiuses & all
sprinckle them with salt and put them in water 2 or . 3
houres, then wash them cleane and set them ouer the
fier, and perboile them scumme them and then doe
soe againe in an other water, soe take 5 quarts of
faire water, and boile them ouer a soft fire, untill
you thinke there be a pottle boiled away, then power
the rest of the water into a cleane bason, and pick
out the snailes out of the shells and with a quart
of hasellnut kernels out of the shells, and blanched
beat the snailes, and soe lett the liquor that they
were last boiled in ouer the fire and put therein
the beaten nuts and snailes with a pound of raisons
of the sunne and a pound of Corrance and boile all
these together with a pound of sugar untill you thinke
the third part be consumed away then straine it
and drinke it warme some 8 spoonsfull at a time
in the morning fasting

67A Medecine for the Piles

Take 2. or . 3 good handsfull of Elder leaues, one of
uiolet leaues, a little rosemary slit, one handfull of
summer sauory, the blades of 2 or 3 heads of house
leeke, . 2 or . 3 branches of orpine, bruise them in a
morter together, and then boile them in 2 or 3 pounds
of fresh butter together untill all the strength of
the same herbes be boiled into the butter, then straine
them as hot as you can through a new canuas cloth
then put it ouer the fier and seath it, scumme it as
you scumme a pot and put therin a good peece of
unwrought waxe, and after it is scummed againe boile it
a while and then sett it in a platter till it be cold and
soe it will be an ointment then take the quantity of
an haselnut and cut it long like a suppositer & put
it in the fundament, and lay a red cloth unto it, and
in soe doing it will ware away





63
68For the biting of a mand
Dogge

Take pennyriall, plantine, Isop, primrose Rootes, and
box, of each a handfull, then take of mustard seed
2 spoonsfull, stampe them together and straine it
forth, with a pinte of new milke from the Cowe and
let the patient drinke it fasting and fast . 24
houres, and then apply the sam hearbes to the sore
adding thereunto a little night shade .
This medicine will cure any beast of the murraine
and many other diseses

69An approued Medicine for
a wett Tetter

Take maiden leekes and hony and wheat flower
and beate them all till they come to an oyle, or thick
like an oyntment or salue, then spread it on a cloath
and warme the wronge side of the cloth or plaister, and
soe lay it on the place griued changing the plaister
twice a day for 3 dayes, and God willing it shall helpe

70A Medicine for a Tetter

Put in the bottom of a brasse Candlestick or other
brasse uessell a little white wine uiniger, and let it
stand . 9 . or . 10 dayes till the brasse rust then put into
it fine powder of bole armeniack and stirre it together
till it come to an oyntment and soe anoint the Tetter
with the same,

72For the Rickets

Take broune fennell, veruaine chickweed, whit Arch Angell
Betony of each a handfull, boile them in faire water, and
bath the childes ribbes and side as hot as he can suffer
it, and doe this once in two dayes, let fluellin be boiled
in all his brothes whereof he must take often





73To heale Children of the lunatique
disease, which happeneth to them
by reason of the worme with 2 heads
that breedeth in their bodies, the
which worme comming to their heart
causeth such a passion in the chil-
dern soe that oft times it killeth them

Take the tender stalkes of a wildingtree, and drie them in
the shadow then stampe them well and sift them, and
take of the powder thereof and roots of Fentian and of
long pyonie of each of them a quarter of an ounce, as
much of myrrhe, all these well beaten to powder, you must
put into a dish or some other uessell and moisten them
with a little water, then take of it with your 2 fingers
and wett the mouth and lips of the child, doe this 3 . or
4 times and you shall see wormes uoided with the Excre-
ment

74An oyntment for the Rickets,

Take Camomill, Time, readsage, Bay leaues, Rue, ueruaine,
white wort, organ mett, wormewood, of each a like quantitie,
powre and boyle them in Butter neuer washed and straine
it, annoint the ribbes and sides of the child with this
This infirmitie is called the welch disease, & whosoeuer
hath had it shall neuer be infected with the plague

75A plaister for the same

Take three quarters of an ounce of English honey, a
quarter of an ounce of mythridate 3 graines of
muske codd, one ounce of oyle of Capers, an ounce of
st Johns wort mixed together, where with you must
first annoint the belly and the thighes and then
lay on a plaister of the same from the bottome
of the belly to the Pitt of the stomack and soe to
the ends of the ribbes

76For a pearle in the Eye

Take a handfull of Pimpernell, of ground Juice of Dasie
roots, of red fennell, of marygolde leaues, of all a like
quantity, stampe and straine them into good ale, and
let the party drinke a good draught, in the morning
fasting, or two houres after meate, and halfe an houre
lye upon his face, after he hath taken it.

77For the Hearing

Take a great onion, peale off the red skinne, cutt of the top
of it, the  take out the inner part, and make a hole in it
and fill it with anisseed, putt on the top close, and wrap it
round with wett broune paper binding it with a threed, and
soe rost it till it be sofft, then straine out the licor and put
a drop into the eare, and stop it with black wooll,

78For great Bellies in man
or woman

Take uiolet leaues, strawberry leaues, browne fennell and
burrage of each a good handfull, licorish and anisseeds of
each an ounce, bruise the anisseed and bruise and slice the
licorish, and then boile all these together in . 2 quartes of
faire water till halfe be consumed, this must be long a
doing on a soft fire then put in an ounce of sene and
let it be couered close together and stand all night by
the fire, then straine it, and put to it an ounce of syrop
of Damaske roses, this quantity being about halfe a pinte
at a time, will serue to be drunke warme, euery morning
for 4 . or . 5 mornings, into uery draught put as much
puluis sanctus as will lye up a 2 for a woman, and as
much as will lye uppon a groate for a man, drinke spring
and fall, or otherwise as there shall be occasion, you may
make this of the rootes if you cannot get the herbs

79A glyster for the stone &
wind Collick

Make a decoction of the seed and leaues of flaxe foenu-
greeke, and mallowe leaues, Althea, Dill, fennell, flowers
of Camomill, Lineseed oyle, Cassia & suger of each an ounce
with . 2 yolkes of egges, and make a glister and iniect
it warme, and hold it 3 . quarter of an houre

80The manner to make your purgation broth
which purgeth malancholly fleagmes
it easeth the collick helpeth therumde
cureth the spleene

Take polypodium of the oake or the Ash which is better, pick
it cleane and bruise it, put it into your water & oatemeale
as you were wont to doe, or if you put to it as much white
wine as water, and . 3 . or . 4 good slices of ginger a few small
raisins, and when your broth is boiled, straine and put to a
draught thereof 2 . or . 3 spoonsfull of syrop of roses and drinke
it fasting and within half an houre after walke to make
it





it worke the better, this will giue you 3 . or 4 stooles at a time,
when you feare to take glister or other strong purgations
which could be dangerous in a weake body without the
aduice of physitions

81For a Bruise

Take of Costmary, parseley, Cherwell of an equall quantity
thred them and stampe them together, with a conuenient
quantity of may butter, and fresh butter and soe being made
into a poultes, apply it to the place hurt

82For falling of the fundament,
bruising, fall, or straine

Take Iowe or flaxe cut it a quarter of a yard in length,
and in breadth as broade as your hand and a pretty thicknes,
annoint the one side thin with a little honey, then strewe
it thick with the powder of whit frankincense, then . 2 whites
of eggs uery well beaten put in that, then let the place
soe grieued be annointed from the fundament upwards
soe broad and so longe as the plaister, with ordinary
Turpentine before the fire a quarter of an houre together
then strewe more powder of frankincense upon the plaister
and so lay it on a double cloth well warmed upon it
and lay it on, and downe upon the back and keepe you
soe warme 4 houres and stirre not nor remove the plaister
before it fall off of it selfe, and if the party haue beene
long grieued use it twice or 3 times in the same manner

83For the hearing

Take Cloues of Garlick and peele of all the filmes, then
steepe them in oyle of bitter allmonds by the space of a
night or a day, then take one out of the oyle, and putting a
threed through the greater end therof, thrust the little
end thereof as farre as you can into your care, then stop
your eare with a little black wooll, and when you take it
out giue it a soudaine twich and immediatly put in an
other, after this manner doe this euery euening and
morning for 9 dayes together, the 9 dayes past put in a
little ciuet in black wooll and stop your Eare with it

84A Precious Seare cloath for the
healing of a running sore

Take oyle of roses . x . ounces of uirgins waxe . 8 ounces
Cerus beaten small . 7 ounces Camfire  smale beaten 3 drams
melt the oyle and waxe together then put in the Cerusse
till it change colour and then  put in the Camfire, so
take it off from the fire dipe the cloth in it

85An oyntment for the Rickets

Take Camomill, time, red sage, bay leaues, Rue ueruaine with
worte . orgamet, wormwood of each a like quantity, powre
and boile them in butter neuer washed, straine it, annoint
the ribbes and sides of the child with this oyntment

86To make yellow oyntment for
all kinde of diseases of the
stomack causes of cold, coughs
or stopings both for old and
younge

Take 4 pounde of fresh butte uery well clariefied,
halfe a peck of broome flowers, as much of the
uppermost leaues or tops of brambles that runne
along the ground, 2 . handfulls of the flowers &
topes of rosemary stampe these in a morter and
boile them uery well together with the butter on a
sofft fire, one houre, and then take it of, and let
it stand in your brasse kettle close couered x dayes
or a fortnight from the dust, then boile it uery
well ouer your sofft fire, untill you see the hearbes
uery well boiled, then straine it and put in a quarter
of an ounce of large mace well beaten, then boile it
softly againe in some other cleane uessell, and put
therto a wuarter of a pinte of aqua Composita melt
2 ounces of unwrought wax therein stirring it till it
be melted, and till such time as nothing appeareth either
of the Juice of the herbes allone, or butter allone but
let it be all one parfect oyntment, then put up as
you please uery close, it will keepe and be good many
yeares, with good usage -
If any haue lack of digestion being with childe or other
wise let them take nuttmegg grated and vinegar and
some of this oyntment melted all together in a saw-
cer, then anoint their stomack with it before the
fire a little while, and when you have used that
which





which is in this sawcer, you must take a peece of cloth
to wipe the nutmegg out of the sawcer and put it
upon the stomack with other hott clothes, using this
for a time, it is uery good to strenghten the stomack

87An oyntment for an Ache .

Take a pinte of neaths foot oyle, halfe a pound of
unwrought waxe, and halfe a pound of whit lead
beate it and searce it uery fine, take 2 penny worth
of white turpentine and 2  worth of camphire, before
you put in your Camphire, you must let the other
boile till it be black

88An Excellent good oyntment
for an Ache in the knees or
any other place of the body

Take . 2 . pound of unsalted butter, a quarter of a pound of
rosemary slipped downewards, a quarter of a pound of
sage, as much of bayleaues, beate these in a stone morter
then boile them together with a soft fire, till all the
Juice be out of them soe straine them through a course
cloth, and sett it ouer the fire againe, put therein an ounce
of each of these following nutmeggs cloues, mace, and
Saffron the weight of 3  dried and finely grounded
to a powder, and a quarter of a pinte of Aqua Composita,
boile all these together and take part of this ointment
and boile with one or two ounces of yellow waxe, soe put
it up, and as occasion serues, spread it as a searecloth
this oyntment is most precious for cold aches, first
to annoint the place grieued with the oyntment, and
then use this seare cloth to it as you see occasion,
sometimes sweating before you apply it, rubbing it uery
well, and drinking in the sweating a pinte of white
wine boiled with a prettie quantity or a handfull of
the tops, of Rosemary, and a good stick of Cinamon,
and some suger with a little saffron dried and
finely bruised

89A uery good oyntment for a sore
breast, bruise, or swelling

Take of southernwood, selfheale, smallage, sage, of
head Bennet, plaintaine, rubbwort, rue, orpin, housleeke
parseley, marygold, mercury, wormwood, Betoney,
scabious, uelaciam, knitwort or Cumphrey, lions
tooth, Buckhorne, of each a handfull, wash them
uery well and put them into a siue to runne dry
when they are uery dry, choppe them uery small
and put thereto a pinte of may butter unwashed
then boile them alltogether untill one part be
boiled away, then strane one part of it to make
ointment salue and put it into a pott, then put
in the other part that is left as much rosen
and waxe, as the quantity of 2 eggs to make it
somewhat stiffe and let them boile together a
good while then straine it and keep it.

90 An Excellent ointment to
make one sleepe

Take halfe a pinte of the best muskadine you can
gett, slice into it an ounce of nuttmeggs then
let it boile or simper till it come to an oyntment
then straine it and spread it upon leather and
lay it on both sides of your forehead upon your
temples, you may use as often as you see cause

91 A uery souueraigne greene
oyle

Take . 2 handfull of henne bane and of hounds tongue
or doggs tongue, Anatampane seu Elicampaine night
shade plaintaine tutson, selfheale and ualerian of
each of them as much as you can take upon yor hand
wipe them in cleane clothes, and seeth them on a
soft fire, in a pottle of sallet oyle, when it is allmost
sudden, put it in halfe a pinte of awuauite, and a
quarter of a pound of unwrought waxe, soe let it
seeth untill it bee greene, then straine it through a
new course cloth, and you may keepe it in a pott close
stopped seauen yeares, this oyle is good for a green
wound to be used for . 2 . or 3 dressings because it is
an





an opener and a cleanser, but for any old ulcer it is
good to be used twice a day, and you shall neuer need
to tent any wound wherein this oyle is put; it is also
good for any other that come with cold, and if any
wound or sore be stopt, scrape some lint and wett it
in the oyle and it will open it againe, the uertue of
this oyle is, when any one take a bruise by falling
or otherwise then to annoint the bruised place
with it warmed, and then lay a thin cloth next
unto it, which must not be remoued from morning
till night, you must keepe the place bruised uery
warme with clothes

92To make oyle of anisseed

Take . 2 poun d of anisseed, bruise it in a morter, then
put it in 4 . hallons of water, and put it in a pot
under the lymbbeck, as you doe for aqua uite, pasting
your pot uery close, and keeping a hotter fire under
it then you doe for Aqua uita, being sure to keepe
the head of your Limbeck cold, the first that comes
out will be strongest, put your water into broad
mouthed glasses, or uessels and let it stand all night
and in the morning with a spoone made for the
purpose take off your oyle, and put it into little
glasses for the use

93To make Black salue

Take a pennyworth of rosin, as much of stone pitch,
turpentine, and hony as much 2  worth of Beese
waxe, melt all these together with some sheepes
or deere suet, this salue is good for any greene
wound or old ulcerated sore whatsoeuer, if there bee
any proud of dead flesh, take the powder of mercury
precipitate, otherwise calld red mercury, and put it into
the wound to cleare it as occasion shall serue, then
dresse it, you may also use Ægyptiacum to cleare
the wound as you see occasion

94To make a poultis for
all swellings

Take . 2 . or . 3 . handsfull of salendine and cut it
small with a knife then boile it in grounds
of ale or beare, and put thereto a good quantity
of sheeps suet with as much wheate branne as will
make it spreade upon a plaster but if it
be for a cold, cause then to bee put in a handfull
of cammomill.

95To make a poultis for all
a white swellings or red
taking

Take grounds of ale or beare, and hollihock
leaues and flowers if you can get them - shred
and boile them in the said licor, put in a good
quantity of sheeps suet shred, while they are
boiling, thicken it with wheaten branne, also
put in the boiling thereof a little urine of a
childe, you must obserue if it be a red taking
that your holihocks be white, and if it be a
white taking that your holihacks be red, spreade
all these upon a plaister and apply it so warme
as the patient is able to endure

96A precious seare cloth for the
healing of a runing sore

Take oyle of Roses . x . ounces uirgins waxe . 8 .
ounces. Cerus small beaten ^ 7 ounces camfere smale .elen 3. drames, melt the
oyle and waxe together, then put in the cerus,
till it can change colour, and then put in the
Camphire, then take it off the fire, and dipe the
cloth therein .

97To make Black salue to heale
old sores, Aches & wounds

Take a quarter of a pound of unwrought waxe
2 ounces of ordinary pitch . 2 . ounces of rosen
2 worth of olibanum, halfe a pinte of sallet
oyle, as much of turpentine, melt all these
together in a little deepe kettle, then take one
handfull





handfull of Jutston leaues one handfull of
Plantin, one handfull of rosemary topes, then
bruise all these herbes in a morter, then put
them in the fore said stuffe . and let all boile
together ouer a sofft fire, with oft stirring
in the seething of it, till it be allmost halfe
sodden away, then straine it through a new
course cloth, soe keepe it good 2 yeares, you must
take it off the fire as it riseth, and when it is
fallen set it on againe, or else it will rune all
ouer the panne, and there will be nothing left
this plaister will not heale the sore thoroughly
till it hath drawne out the dead flesh and all the
corruption, then it will heale throughly without
any other salue

98A Balsom to cure any cult
greene wound or ulcer

Take the leaues of Plantin, Angelica, Rosemary saint
Johns wort, of each a handfull boile all these in . 3 .
pints of running water, untill the halfe of the water
be consumed then put in halfe a pinte of oyle of
greene unripe oliues, called oyle Omphacium, of cleare
and bright Turpentine . 4 ounces . of Frankincense
finely beaten one ounce of yellow waxe one ounce and
a halfe boile all these together a small space with
a soft fire, then straine out the liquor, and when it
is cold, put it up in gelly potts, and keepe it as a frend
at need
This Balsom will most speedily and safely cure any
cutt, greene wounde how deepe soeuer, or any ulcer being
first cleansed.

99To write upon a plate of
Iron or knife .

Take of Aquafortis one ounce, dissolue in it a quarter of
an ounce of mercury sublimate, then take uirgins waxe,
and melt a little on the Iron plate or knife, and being
cold write or draw on it with a needle what you please,
then with a pencill brush, first dipped in that water
rubb ouer the plate or knife, and let it stand . 2 houres, or
a night, if you will haue it deepe.





68
100To make the Scuruy grasse
drinke, according to Doctor
Colaton his Prescription

Take the roots of munkes Rhubarb the roots of redd madder
of each halfe a pound, Agrymony, scabious, of each . 4 hands
full, anisseed and licorish of each . 3 ounces, sere half
a pound slice the rootes, breake the hearbes with your hands
and bruise the seeds; and put all these in a bagg in a
uessell of 4 gallons of Ale and beare together that it
may not lye att the bottom thereof, soe let it steepe
4 or . 5 dayes, before you drinke it, a good pinte in the
morning, will purge uery well, and if it should faile
in any, let them take halfe as much more in the after
noone,

Finis
