If to cleare e eyes & stopp Rhume in them, then morne
inge, and euening dropp a little of it into them and; mixeinge
with it a very little of e salt of damaske rosees, red fennell
or red sage; and thies make e salt of damaske roses; take the
rose leaves power e water is distilled out of them; burne them to
ashes, after calcine them in a Crusible, then steepe them in
a quart or bottle of the water, that is distilled out of them
5 or 6 daies till e saltnesse bee fetcht out of them; then
straine or filter the water through a browne or graye
paper untill it bee very cleare; then take e water, and
putt it into a glasse still, or greate high glasse wthout a Couer
Boill it in Balnco untill e liquid bee all gone; then take
the salt wch is in e bottom; and keepe it in a glasse or Bell
lose couered neare the fire.
Another for the eyes. Mr. Stepkin.
Take right venice fawlke, grynde him to powder
upon a painters stone, put it into  e eye to strengthen it
or to take of any tender seurn, or to take of thicke skin
or scume of the eye: mixe of it with liue honey & powder
of eggshells, not boiled nor washed but rawed; onely bee
sure to take away the thin skinn or filme on  e inside
and soe brake it to powder and searce it through a uery
fine searce, and applye sometyme of it selfe  e powder of
e eggshells, and sometymes mixed wth  e honey, not warm enight.

? To make Mathiolus Cynumon water
Take twoe pound of lardge Cynamon; burise & putt to it
eight pintes of Rosewater, and fower pintes of spanishe
wine; Put altogher in Balneo Matur; stop it close that

an halfe of fine white sugar.

Drinke halfe a pinte at a draught; morneinge, after
noone, and eveinge to bed three daies together./

An excellent thinge to cause sleep in feauers
Take the white of an egg well beaten till if bee oile; spreade
that oile on a lenen cloathe; aud at night when y goe to bed clapp
this on your foreheade ouer your temples; ett if bye on all night
this neuer failes; I tryed it upon myselfe in affeauer; whe
I culd not sleepe for many nights till Itryed this

A most rare Receipt for a Burne.
Take three spoonefull of sallett oile; then take ye white jam

Dr: Wrights Diett drinke for ye dropsee
Take 4 ounces of shaueings of Ivory, china roote sliced smale
twoe ounces, Egremory newly dryed 4 handfulls; salsafras wood
sliced thin three ounces dryed Orange Pills Cytron Pills
grossely beaten of each one handfull; wheatebran one handfull
and an halfe; Roman wormewood dryed 2 handfulls, ppared
coriander seedes, Gallingall Rootes, Caraway seedes, grossly
powdred of each three drachmes; mixe all theise together
and with fower gadds of new steele, put them into a Bagg
to hange in the meddle of a Lundlett of fower or fiue
gallons, and turn it up in the vessell full of good Ale
this is to bee taken at Meales, and as often as you please
three Baggs will serve twice.

The Bath for Leggs.

handfulls, germander, groundpine, seawind weede ahkean
handfull. seward barke of Ashetree ,Bayberries, Juniper berries
and Cumin seedes each an handfull and an halfe ppare theise &
boile them in 15 quarters of water to ten, and soe resevare it for
your bathe.

 The powder for the fume

Caciamahae three drachmwa, rosemary an handfull ligne Alores

The Covroborateinge powder.
Take sage, water germander, wormwood, germander, bettame
of each twoe handfull; flowers of rosemary, Red roses, & stach bades
each two handfull; Beane meale & Lupines ether aounce, Bay
berries, Juniper berries & mirblees, each an ounce & an halfe
mesbe & Amber ether halfe an ounce, soe make upe powder
My way of useinge them at night iust before shee went to bedd; shee
sate with her leggs in the bathe up to fe garterings places for an
houoer, as best as shee could well endaier; then with her cloathes
well smoaked with fe  fume, was fe  leggs dryed & smoaked; then
Psently was shee gott to bed; and her leggs wth other drye cloathes.

Dor: Whittmores receipt for the Lozenge.

Take of Candid Cytron pills goinge, Lemon pill of each twoe drachmes
of Candied ginger, a drachme and an halfe, of erengo rootes candied
three drachmes, of Blaunchd Almonds twelve. of cloves & gallingall
of each ten graines; cutt them all very smale with a Knife; then
take 2. ounces of loafe sugar, & putt to some 2. or 3. ounces
of rose water, and boile the sugar till it come almost to e height
then put in e things & Last of all three graines of Ambergreece
and twoe of muske. after you have stirred it well together
powre it forth upon a Lye plate & cutt it into Lozenges. This
very good to strengthen yoour Stomach and very useful against a
rawe wh stomach, used by Mee. R.H.

The Balsom of Luetellas & the vertues thereof
as it is now made, but not after his pscripcon; for that
is formerly in another place of this Booke; given
mee by Sr. J Monson
Melt 4 ounces of scraped origins waxec put to it a pound &
an halfe of pure Oile Olive beaten  a pinte of Canara sack
put into a skellett on the fire, & beinge ready to boile; putt into it
one ounce and an halfe of red saunders in powder, and twoe ounces of
naturall Balsome; fowre ounces of  oile of St Johns wort
couchaneale halfe an ounce finely powdred, pure honey fower
ounces spermaceti fower ounces; mill and incorporate them
well together as they are boileinge; And lastly putt to it one ounce
of right venice Turpentine that hath beene beaten & washt
in severall damask rose waters, untill it bee pure white
Then boile them all a longe tyme, and take speciall Care it
runn not over; then take it and straine into a silver
Bason or Galley pott; and when when it is cold cutt a peece of
it downe to the very bottom; powre out all the water, and
keepe it in a glasse Bottle; for it is good to wash a wounde
with all, and then melt the Balsome; and here followinge
are the vertues of it.
1. It cureth the headache by molteinge & chafinge it into the
temples, and annointeinge the inside of the nostrills there
2. It cures the biteinge of a mad dogg, the loose knarley flesh being
cutt away, it cures greene woundes, Burnes, Bruises, paines
and Aches.
 3. Bones.





3. Bones, sinnues, orassy paine or griefe comminge onely of cold causes
by moltringle it, & annointinge the wound all ouer wth it, spread
it cold upon plaisters, and bynde it all over e sores, dresseing it
once a day, for wounds & plastings, probat.
4. It is good against winde Collicke, or stitch in the syde applyed
there unto warme with hott cloathes, foeer morneings a quarter
of an hower together.
5. It helpeth and cureth vlcers, or ffistuloes bee it neuer soe deepe in
any part of tho body beinge there unto applyed.
6.It is good for any infected .with the plague, meazells or smale
poxe, presently take in warme broathe, a quarter of an hower
fower mornings together, sweateinge upon it.
7.It is good against poyson, helpeth a surfit, takeinge one ounce of
it in warmed or hott sacke.
8. It staucheth bleedinge in greate wounded ; takinge flaxe or longe
Linte & putting into it, and soe tyied hard unto it.
9.It helpeth digestion by annountinge the Nauell & stomach therewith
at goeinge to led ; chafeinge it warme before the fire.
10.It helpeth and easth the spleene; spread upon cow leather, and
apply to the place
11. It healeth the biteinge of madd doggs, & vennomous beastes beinge
both outwardly & inwardly applyed.
12. ffore a wound wash a bullett, warme it very hott & powre it into the

13. Its good for a scald or burne, beinge dressed herewith as hott as may

14. It helpeth hoarsenessed and cold, and its good for a consumption

The magnsteriall pill for the spleene./
Take of diacathelicon halfe an ounce, of the tincture of
rhubarb a scruple, of the extraction of Cetracke a drachme
of creame of tarter a scruple, of salt of wormewood, & salte
of scuruly grasse of each one scruple, of prpared Cassia a
scruple; Beate all these together, and make them up into
fortie pills with the flower of Liquorice, and take fiue of
them in the morneinge, and fast twoe or thee howers after
Then take a meale of broathe wch must bee made with openinge
hearbes and Rootes, and an hower after againe, take
another mease of the same broathe then dine abbout
twelve a clocke; and after dinner you may goe abroade
when you goe to bed you must take five more of these pills
and soe





and for on till you have taken all thaise Pills; yo drink must
bee one spoons full of wormewood beere or, Ale; and drink noe
other drink for seauen or eight daies; and eate veale, Chicken
or Rabbets stew or boiled.
The Cordiall Pills
Take of Alkermes twoe drachmes, of tincture of Corrall
one scruple of drye sucket of Cytron pills twoe drachmes, one
scruple of the conserue of Centaura, fiue graines of Bezor
one scruple of the fine powder of prpared pearle; make
this up into pills: Giue three in the morneinge; walk an
hower after; take three more at twoe of the clocke, and
three more at night; theise must bee thus taken for three
daies together, and they will both seettle the stomach and express
winde, and strengthen the heart; theise in the morneinge
must bee taken fasteinge.

A medicine for the plague as a peseruatiue

Take hearbe grase, aetherwood, Mugwort, wormewood of
each one handfull; three or fower handfull of Juniper
berries, those that are blacke; Cutt and shread  hearbes
smalle, put them into a fflaunders Pipkin; power
there to 4 quartes of white wine vinegar; and lett
then soake seauen nights; if the hearbes beedry; if they
bee greene lett them not soake abboue 24 howers; then
putt one spoonehandfull or two of theise hearbes wch bee
in the Pypkin into a still; then take out a quart of
the same vinegar, and power it out into e still
upon the hearbes, and distill it wth a grutle fire
for twoe greate a fire will wast it awaie; and soe still
it untill you haue stilled all from the Pypkin into
the stillatory; and after this power it into your
Pypkin againe; and putt as many fresh and newe
hearbes with soe many berries as you had before into
the Pypkin againe; and soe lett them soake
other seauen nights, and then still them againe
as you did before; and soe againe the third tyme

with the same fresh hearbes, and berries, and att

the pipkin amongst the hearbes; or five or sixe

stop it and bynde a leather abbout it, and if e patient

Cause; to a woman give but twoe ounces at a tyme, and
to Children accordinge to their age, as they are able to
beare it, and sweate after it twoe or three howers, and one
to stand a houres by & wipe their faces; least by puttinge
out their owne hand they take cold.

A medicine to drawe the venome from t heart
afor the sore appeareth.

Take a Cocks pullett, or chicken; let the feathers of the taile
be quite pulled of, till the rumps bee bare; then how the bare
of the pullett to the sore; and the pullett will gape & labour
for life and in the end will die; then have another
readie & doe the how with it, and if it dye dtill apply
more for longe as any die; and when all the poyson is
drawne forth, the chicken that is put thereto will
live; and the sore will psently asswadge; and the
party forth with recovereth.

The Plaister to draw a head to the sore
of the plague, and to break it

Take twoe lillie rootes, one handfull of soure dough
twooe handfull of Mallowes, one handfull of Linseede

Laie it an inch thicke upon leather, broader then
the sore; and lett the borders or ends of the leather
bee plaisterd with Cordeirs or Coblers waxe
to make it cleane and riche, and this is
twelve





twelue howers will drawe the sores to a head and breack it
A plaister to drawe and heale the sore
Take twoe spoonefulls and an halfe of the iuce of

To make pilla Rupha to puent the plague.
Take of Aloes saccatvina one ounce, of myrrhe halfe an
ounce, of saffron halfe an ounce; Theise must bee all finely
powdred but the Aloes must bee ppared thus
Take an ounce of syrrupe of Lemmons, in wch infuse the
Aloes beinge powdred, untill you see e Aloes sincke
to the bottom and settled; then powre away e syrrupe
and mixe the saffron & myrrh with the Aloes, and make
it vp into a Masse of wch  pills make one or twoe of
the bignesse of a smalle pistoll bullett; wch  take to purge
the heade one hower one hower after supper; if for
the body twoe howers before dinner, if in yr tyme
of infection assoone as you vse in the mornenige
A soueraigne water for a Cancker
Take wormewood, sage, Pennywall, red filbert leaues &
rue of each one handfull, wash them cleane, dry them
in a cloathe; then putt them in a pan with twoe quartes

of honey; sett it on te fire, couer it close; and lett itt
boile leasurely to a quart; then draine it, and keepe
it for your vse, other to gargle with itt, or to
washe the place.

For a sore Arme or Legg that is Blistered

A plaister to heale one that is bursten in e  belly
man, woman, or Childe.
Take tender cropps  of Olmond in May and dry them
in the shade, powder them smale; Keepe it in a bladder
an winter and and sommer put it into yor drinke, this will
make the broken belly as greene as if it were new broken
and the patient must bee well but gently trussed; that
hee or shee may walk up and downe all the day withitt
and lye in it all night till they bee well and whole; wch
wilbee  within 24 daies; if they bee well ordered &
ruled; and they must lye upright as much as they can
and lye still in their beds 2 howers euervy morninge
after they have drunken this Medicine; and  wak must
eate noe white meates, and walke but easily for feare
of  straineinge; untill they bee poerfect well againe

The plaister to heale one that is broken.

Take the rootes of polypodium upon the Enula Campana roote
of each fower ounces pare them cleane, & stampe them smale
with a little Comfrey amongst it; then temper it with oile
de Bay & alittle Capons grease; then spread it on a thicke clath
and lace it to the treened place under the trusse, chaunge it
eveninge & morneinge, & it will helpe suddenly; it strengtheneth
all the yeare longe but only in the monrthe of May; and in that
monrth take the leaves of polypodium, Enula Campana, Comfry
and daysirs leaves, wringe them in all pointes dareinger that moneth
as you doe the rootes the  & rest of of the yeare

>The receipt to make the oile of
Massicke to fortify & strenghten
weak womb and limbs./
Take Mastic & bruise it in a Morter, put it in a quart
glasse with halfe a pinte of Malmesley, and a pinte of
good oil of Roses, then couer the glasse & sett it in a Pan
of boileing water, & lett if boile till the wine bee consumed
then take it from thee fire &  lett it stand till it bee cold, and reserue
it for yourr use; applyeinge it to the greeued place, & chafeinge it
in with a warme hand.
An oile for greate Aches or  shrunksinnewes
Take garden wormes in May, put them in a vyoll or Pott
cover them over with Rhenish wine, stop the glasse or Pott
fier not uery slow; lett it stand 24 howers and in that
tyme they will purge all the earthly substance from them
putt them into another glasse, and cast away the rest
then take thee best & greenest sallett oile you can gett, and
powre it upon them; stopp them uery close that noe aire
goes out. sett it in the sunne all day, and at night couer
it with a wooden cloathe; doe this in the twoe monthes
of June and July; and then it is readie made; annointe thee
greeued place twice a day, and dry it by the fire untill
the Patient finde ease; which wilbee in a short tyme as
hath beene often prooued.
A Drinke for a hott Liuer
Take a pound of red Currans, boile them in a quart
of runninge water till they be halfe sodden away, then
straine them well, and take the iues of three
Lemmons, and put it in after it is strained, and three
ounces of fine sugar, and soe drinke it in thee morninge
fastringe; at three a clocke in the afternoone, and
againe at yourr goinge to bed

take asmuch honey as will make it fitt to spreade upon a
cloathe.

foie away, and after use a healinge salue to it

To heale a wound most Excellent
Stampe snails very smale, temper them with the powder of
Myrrhe, ffranekincence, Rosin and the dust of a Milne
laie it to the greife & it will close & heale it, though the
syneoes were broken in sunder the roote of Comphey in
powder, cast into the wound or mixt with the salue is
very execellent for greene wounds, as hath ben approued
ffor tho paene at the heart.
Boill one handfalll of haire hound in a quart of good ale
till it bee tender, then chopp it smale and boile it againe
and when it hath boiled a good whyle, put to it one handfull
of good to wheate meade, lett it boile againe, then take it of
spreade it on a cloathe, and laie it on e stomach hott.

ffor the Palsey By Mr Turner
Bonell a Badger of ffoxe rost it in his Belly sage rose=
mary, Angelica of each a good quantity, as much of hearbe
Mariorun, Cowrslipps & Bettony beinge first chopped with
the fatt & leane of the gutts, then take Nutmegg, ginger, loins
Mace  Castov^eum & put them into the Belly; before yu savce
it vp, bye bothends to the spitt, to prevent vent and bathe
it only with his owne drippinge; the pan bring first halfe
full of vinegar; when it is halfe rosted; pricke it that the
iuice may come out of the Belly & other places, & rost it till
it will dripp noe more; keepe the oile close stopt in a
glasse; sett it in y^e sunne, & annointe with it

Auery excellent broath to stay loosenesse.
Take halfe a greate Cocke Chicken, & boile it, then put in
strawberry leaues & rootes, plantaine, barnett, & fiue leaueed
grasse, feach one hanfull; Boile them well together, then take
a good quanttity of the meate & seede of a Pomgranete, put it into
the broathe untill you thinke it swell, & soe use it.
Another for the same
Take twoe quartes of milke, when it doth seeth putt into

then put in halfe an ounce of Cinomon, let it boile halfe a pinte
away; then put in twoe spoone full of Beane flower; let it boile
straine, & drinke it; this hath beene prooued.

A soueraigne water for e eyes
Take smaladge, red fennell Rhue, Verain, Bettany. egrimony
Giner foile, Pennyroyall, Cellandene, of each a quarter of a pound
Beate them in a Morter smale, then take fifteene pepper Cornes
beate them uery fine; put all into a faire pan, wth three
spoons full of honey fiue spoonefull of the urine of a
younge childe, halfe a pinte of whitewine; boile all a little
on e fire, then staine it through a fine cloathe; when it
is colde putt it unto a glasse ; and annointe the eyes with a
ffeather within the lidd; this is good for an hard wateringe
eyes, and all sore eyes.
For e Pinn & webb the eyes.
Take the pinion bone of a goosewinge, the longer it
hath beene killed tho better it is, melt it in a cleane
Sawcer ; when it is cold putt one dropp into your
eyes when you go to bed; use it till your eyes bee well
which wilbee in a short space.

To make syrrupe of damask Roses
for high coloured faces

Take foure pound of faire water make it blood warm
putt in half a pecke of dammask Roses lett them stand
34 houers, then wring them out hard; then warme
the water againe, and putt in annother half pecke of
Roses, lett them stand as before; then wringe them
out, doe soe fouoer mourings; then boile one quarter
of a pound of sugar in it soehigh till it come to the
thicknesse of molten honey; this is good for an high
coulored face, and for the heate of the liver; After
the same manner you may make syrrupe of vyoletts wh
are good for the uses abbovesaid; both taken inwardly
and to anointe the face therwith. Probatum.

The preparation or makings of the spirit of Rue
bring a greate cordiall excellent to preserve youthe
in old age with fresh & good coulour without wrinckles
or shewe of age, and agt the plague, pox, or any venomous
or contagious infection, taintes of the blood, and good
agt all manner of goutes, but chiefly agt the old goute
to bee taken fastings; its good also against all
obstructions, & good upon every surfeit.

You must gather the e in a dry day in June; & hott
betwixt eight a clock and twelve, chopp it small
bringe first cleane picked; putt it in a large glasse
body ot still of glasse, fill it almost halfe full not
pressed downe hard; then putt on the head of  t e
glasse and late it, and sett it in Balnco Maria-
and a cleane decanter of it of glasse, and gently distill it
till it bee almost dry; But you must not distill it too
much. last that it smell of burneingr; that
which remaines behind take out cleane &[and] throwe
it away, and putt as  much fresh, picked and







and chopped Rue as at the first tyme and powre the
water you distilled from it vpon the freshe Rue; & lute the heade
putt to the Receuier and distill it as a fore; Destill this
alsoe the third tyme with fresh rue; then take all the
spiritt foe distilled three tymes from the Rue, and putt
that spirit into a cleane glasse Bowl; & sett it in Balme
Maria; then lute & put on the head; & putt a Receuier
of glasses & distill it gently; leaueinge one fourth partop
it behinde; that is distill awail three partes, and then
lett it coole; and that whh remaines in the bottom cast away
for the other is the true spirit; if you will once distill
or rectify that againe in a cleane glasse; leaueinge the
fourth part of that behinde; you haue the a moste
excellent spirit of Rue; ffor the glasse wherein you
keepe it, hangs at the bottom of the Corke that stoppst the
glasse some ambergrease ground with a little sugar candy
in a sarsnett Bagg; the proportion of it to to bee taken is
4 or 5 spoons fulls fasteings, (in th) some sugar candy

Tak a little Ointment which you shall laue at the

The plaister of the same.
Take halfe a pound of porrasen, one quarter of a pound of
yellowe waxe, twoe ounces of storax, calamita, a quarter
of an ounce of Beniamin, 4. penny waights of cloves, halfe a
pound of hoggs suet clarified; three quarters of an ounce of fine
Bolearminicke, a quarter of an ounce of oile of spyke, shaue e
waxe alsoe, beatee rossin smale, & e storax smale as  ycan
Brate alsoe the Beniamin & Bolearminick, soare them, &
boile them altogether a quarter of an houer; then put in the
oile of spike, & soe make it up in rolls; first annoint ye hands
with butter, as it will cleaue soe to them that e cannot get it of.
Ffurther directions for the spleene.
Vse to rubb the napp of e necke with twoe dropps of the
spirits of Rosemary, soe it be wise yor temples; rememberinge
often to repulse the humores with your hand in the morninge.
Other directions for the same.
If yor plaister on your side doe waxe dry at any tyme
then moysten it with a little oile of Capers.
Other directions for the same
Confectio Alkermes is necessary to bee taken often
to quenche and abbate the the Melloncholly vapours that
arrise from the spleene.
Ffurther directions for e same
Take a quarte of the pickle of Olives; cleane it finely
then foment the left side twoe or three tymesd an
houre at a tyme; after Gatheringe annointe e side with e
ointment made of unguentum de Altea, and oiles of
Carawaies together; then put on the plaister abbove
mentioned.

Horshegreene sicknesse given me by J.H

Take a quart of milk and a quart of white wine, make
a possett; take a pinte of earthe wormes, put them
into white wine; untill they are cleansed therein
then take of the curd from the possett, putt the wormes
in, and lett thenboile two or thrree soalmes in an earthen
pott, and drink halfe a pinte at adraught twice a day
for ten daies together; make fresh every day

Angell breade for a consumption given mee by
the L Ha taught her by Mr Coax

Take pure wheat flower , putt it in a gallet pott some
halfe an inch deepe; then take white sugar candy brate it
small and laie another layre of that the same deeenesse
of the flower; follow this methode untill y have filled
the pott; then cover it close and sett it in an oven
after browne bread is drawne ; lett it stand forer
and twentie houers then take it out and if you can







can gett out your breade ; if not break yor Pott carefully
toe take it out, Brake the breade in a Morter very fine; & then
searse it; and to a pound of this powder add a quarter of
an ounce of prpared pearle; Give it in milke, Barley
broath or what you like; To a man give twoe spoonefulls at a
tyme; and to a Chide one spoonefull at a tyme.
Another for a Consumption given mee by the L H
Take a gallon of new wort before the yest is put to it; Boile
it in an Iron pott till it bee consumed to a pinte; Keepeinge
it scuminge as longe as any froath will rise up from it; this
must bee done with a constant gentle fire; put it into a
galley pott; when it is done; every night, morninge, and
afternoone; take as much hereof as will lye upon a Knifes pointe
it must bee longe in boileinge & not too suddenly.
A very good Almond milke to coole & quallifie
heate of e stomach, or liver, & good in Agues
Take one handfull of french barley in a pott be of faire spounge
water untill the water dissolve and twine of a red coulour; then
shift it and put in foroer or five parsley rootes, and twoe or 3.
fennell rootes washt and seraped; and the pithes taken out
and when they have boiled a good while, put in theise hearbes
followeinge as you finde the sicke bodies temper; if hott, hysope
& horehound;  if cold, Burrage, vyolitt, succoury & endive leaves
when the water is halfe consumed beate yor Almonds with
twoe or three spoonefulls thereof, or with red rose water
then drawe them with the rest of the broathe, and putt in
some sugar; you may alsoe boile a whole peecie of Cynamon &
eight or ten raisons of the sunne stones with the hearby
if it bee a hott feaver the Cynamon is not good.

To make juice of liqice a very fine waie.

Take fower ounce of english liquorice finely beaten and
searced, put it into hysop water and Coltsfoote water of
each a lid quantity till it bee as thicke as butter, addinge
alsoe to it halfe a spoonefull of rosemary flowers bruised
Set it on the fire, let it boile softly till it bee as thicke as tars
meale, stirringe it very often; then straine it through a thicke
new cloathe as longe as you can get any thinge out of it ; then
Sett it on the fire againe, and put to it foure ounces of white
or brown sugar candy finely beaten; boile it till it come to a
paste; then roll it up in balles and sett it by the fire, and
Keepe it for your use.

A Purgeinge Pill: L: H:
Take halfe an ounce of Myrth, one ounce of Aloes, one drachme
of saffron; Beate theise sewally & searce them; then take a
quantity of the syrrupe of Roses with Agaricke, and twoe
dropps of oile of annesseeds; make it up into past, and then
into pills; take one in the morneinge fasteing; and halfe an
hower before supper, and you are nether tyed to dyet nor to keepe
the house.           Dr: Heareinge.

ffor a tinckleinge Cough. L. H.

foure and twentie howers; after still them in Balneo
Mari&aelig; Tacke a quarter of a Pinte at a tyme ith a little
sugar morninge and eueninge/

Against pisseinge ones bed, giuen the L: Hu:
By Mr Cooke
Take the leafe called Mouseare, gathered in sommer
dry itt, and beate it to powder; Giue the partie asmuch
as will lye upon a shillinge in porrenge, or what other
thinge yow please euery morninge; This will
Certainely helpe and Cure./

contained in the Lady

Bread Pudding with MS. 363

Grate 1/2 a H of stale Bread - Pour over it 1 pint of hot milk and
leave the mixture to soak for an hour in a covered basin -
Then beat it up with the contents of 2 eggs -
Out the whole into a covered basin just large enough to hold it
which must be tied in a cloth and placed in boiling water for
1/2 an hour - It may be eaten with Salt Sugar of Sherry Wine
Ex Culverwell's guide to Health & [??]
Pub: by Sherwoods
23 Pat'r Row/.

Parmado

Place some very thin slices of crumb of Bread in a saucepan and
add withe more water than will cover them. Boil until the Bread
becomes pulpy. then strain off the superfluous water and beat up the Bread
until it becomes of the consistence of gruel - then add white sugar and
when permitted a little sherry wine
